Owner (v147): "i want something between h2 and h3 -- i like the loosening up, but the coffee cup plate feels too far. and the flares too intense. the museum looks completely out of place, so there needs to be a pattern for bringing this to the more structural icon decisions. also the interior counter shape of the coupe *still* does not look right. and there is *still* not enough liquid in the cup how many times do i have to say it."

    - The wall beside the band measures 1.50 / 1.50 / 1.50 across and 1.48 / 1.47 / 1.44 square.
    - Trade, stated on the board: some of the flatness he picked.
  - **(b) An open glass:** B's silhouette with no bar across the rim. The mouth is the clear part (1 1/6). Its sides are the bowl's curve moved in by the line.

    - Wall beside the mouth: 1.49 / 1.50 / 1.50 across.
  - **(c) The drink as its own shape:** an outline glass, with the drink a separate shape inside it and a 5/6 gap all round.
    - Drink 7.2px², 41% more than v134.

    - Trade, stated on the board: the flat bowl (it's rounder and deeper).
- **Not shown:** a literal crescent under the rim. It pinches to slivers where it meets the walls (the no-ink-traps rule).

**2. Between H2 and H3** (row 5 of HSETS; the v144 boards keep their four rows).
- **Plates and feet:** a slab whose top rises 1/2px at the centre, so its ends are 1px and its crown 1.5. H3's dome came down to a point at the ends.
- **Flares:** 3/4px, half of H3's 1.5.

- Columns get a capital and a base: the slots' corners round to 0.5, a third of the slot. That is under the outside's 0.6, so slots don't become pills.
- The outer columns meet the beam and plinth with the same 0.5.
- Slab ends take the line-end round.
- A base on the ground stays flat.
- It governs Hotel, Neighbourhood and Venue. Landmark's ⌘ loop is a sign, not a building.

- Curve check: 0.00/px at non-end joins (the café 0.41).

## Turn 106: design director, v204: the museum belongs; one fix at the eave tips, then 9 and send

**His canvas items:** I diffed against my saved v203 copy. "Artboard
1", "Decide: line marks copy" and his note are identical; only the
team's boards moved. Pass.

**The museum at 24px and 12px:**
- **It reads as a museum.** Roof, four slender columns and a base. With
  no beam the openings run up to the roof, so the columns read taller
  and the colonnade rhythm is back. It's no longer a box with doors.

  eye. That matches the blur (mean 29.3, darkest fifth 62.3, against
  the cup's 28.3 and 70.6).

  building sheds a part rather than its rhythm") passes G-derive. A
  hotel or a shop front can follow it without losing what makes it
  recognisable.
- **Pass.**

**The eave tips at 8x: a fault.**
- With the beam gone, each eave is a sliver.
  - The roof's slope meets its underside 0.25px outside the column, in
    a point with a round of about 0.04px (the line-end curve figure
    26.67/px, radius about 1/27px).
  - At 8x it reads as a small barb hooking down. At the 24 badge it
    shows as a speck at each eave end.
- **It breaks two of our own rules:**
  - the corner law (outside points 0.35);
  - the no-thin-points rule, which the board itself cites to label the
    coupe crescent.

## Turn 115: icon designer, turn 114 fixes in one batch (canvas v215)

1. **The vessel rule.**
   - New in picks7: `VESSEL_SIDE` {bk 0.5, bx 1.5, lean 0}. A curved side is one curve from the rim, with no upright run and no S. That is the Between coupe's fix; until now it lived only inside that row's options.
   - The tuned row now spreads it into its coupe, so any row can take it.
   - Measured on the coupe side, least curvature over peak: 17% → 38%.

     - cup bottom 44%, tree underside 49%: fine;
     - the saucer's underside (2%) and the foot's top (5%) run flat across the middle.

   - what rises into a stem domes up to meet it (the foot);

## Turn 124: icon designer (third), the museum: building the flaring in (canvas v220)

Owner: "When I say connected I mean to the systemic approach. I like the separated gable … it's the flaring effects that the museum and other structures like it aren't going to naturally make. How might we build some of that in?"

- **Where the flaring lives in the coupe and the tree:** where a post meets a mass (the stem into bowl and foot, the trunk into its tier), where a mass ends in the air (a tier hangs past the one below, its underside falling to its tip), and in an outline that widens to the ground. A building has all three places; it is only drawn so no rule reaches them. Each approach is one rule that reaches one place.
- **The base first (turn 123's fixes):** picks7 `structMuseum` (`o.struct`). The separated gable is kept and rests on the beam across the 1px cut; beam, columns (1 1/4) and plinth are one piece; the columns are full height, taller than the roof. The cut's room comes from a lower roof (`gb 4, ap 1.333`), never from the supports. Resting parts now carries the limits in the rule: at most one cut per icon; both parts read whole; the cut never shortens the supports. It is the row's museum (R2, R2B). Still the WIDE class (a SQUARE fit was tried: no visible gain).
- **A · Joins** (`cap`, `foot`): an outer post flares 1/2px into the beam and the base on the OUTSIDE, as the stem does; past the post the beam and base thin to 1px ends (the plate rule). Openings stay square. Future building: corner walls flare into cornice and base; windows square.
- **B · Outline** (`batter 0.6`, `sweep`): the outer posts' outside edges sweep out to the base in one concave curve, as the tree and the coupe's foot widen downward; the openings' sides stay upright. Future: outer walls sweep out at the foot.
- **C · Ends** (`eave`): the roof passes the beam by a shelf (0.9 unit); under it the underside falls 0.4px to a 1px end, as a tier's underside falls to its tip. Slopes straight. Future: any roof, awning or cornice hangs and dips.
- **How I got there:** a real draw (shuf) gave "root flare at a trunk's base", "bookbinding", "joinery". The root flare pointed at the base (A's foot, B); type's bracketed serif (a stem bracketed into a projecting serif, never into the counter) gave "flare on the outside only".
- **Dropped:** the roof ending in a point (a sliver under the line, the v204 barb again); a straight batter (a trapezoid base, his 10-20 root-shape note); flares inside openings (pills); a steeper roof for a deeper droop (tent / house).
- **True size:** each changes the 24-badge outline; at the 12px chip the differences are under a pixel and the cut greys to half a pixel (said on the board). Blur (overall / darkest fifth): base 28.5/61.1, A 28.2/61.0, B 29.3/61.4, C 28.6/62.6; cup 28.4/70.8. All four sit at the cup's weight, so "sized by its weight" is off the board (it remains in the archived v219 board).
- **Board:** one museum section replacing v219's: his quote, where the flaring comes from, v219 beside the base, A/B/C each with rule + future building + row + chip/24/72/8x + weight, one true-size paragraph, one question ("Which of these, if any, connects the museum to the coupe and the tree for you?"). v219's board is on Archive as `Archive-G7am-Between` (x 3360, y 156200). ## Turn 129: icon designer (fourth), joins and optical centring as engine rules (canvas v223)

Owner (v222): "vertical centering should be optical always on a per-icon basis. the join patterns for sharp corners like this tree don't seem to be the same quite yet at a pattern level ... a slight wobbliness at some of the joins because of the rounding mechanics ... they should be architectural and smooth with no visible humps or wobbles... they should feel impeccably constructed at every join." And: "im still concerned that the counter edge looks less curved than the exterior edge".

**His marks, identified:** `v222-tree-joins` = the tree's left side (two plain-arc upper tips beside an eased bottom tip); `wobble-a` = the coupe's foot (end corner, the foot's top, into the stem); `wobble-b` = a stem flare meeting the bowl's curved underside (the museum's capital is the same join); `wobble-c` = the coupe's rim corner into its side (a flat spot right after the corner); `coupe-band-edge` = the band's end, a short straight cut beside the curved wall.

**Causes, in the engine (`corners()`):**
- The ease was chosen by edge LENGTH (`min(lin, lout) >= 3`), so corners of one kind split: the tree's apex and bottom tips eased, its upper tips plain arcs.
- Every round assumed its edges were straight: handles from the corner's angle, curvature 0 at the round's ends. Where the edge was a curve, the round ended flat and the edge bent again (the rim), or a circular arc met a curve with a curvature step (flares, the foot).
- A corner running straight into a bend the other way (the museum's beam end into its capital) was rounded as a 90° corner: a knuckle.

**The three rules (all in the engine, `opts.join`; boards7 `JOIN7`, `opticalY`):**
1. **Optical centring, per icon:** the box centre, pulled down by half the distance the ink sits above it; a drawing heavy at the foot is not lifted. Exact, no 1/3px snap. The star and + by the same rule in their slots. Effect: cup up 0.10px, coupe up 0.09, museum down 0.26 (it read high), tree down 0.10, star down 0.37.
2. **One join pattern:** by kind, never length. Every outside corner of a mass (tip, rim, apex) eases; every inside corner, flare, notch, counter and line end takes one compact round (two mirrored cubics through the law arc's deepest point). The tree's three tiers now end alike.
3. **No humps:** every round is solved to meet its edges with their own tangent and curvature and to bend evenly (one peak). Where that can't be solved, the round slides along the curved edge until it can; where a corner runs into an opposite bend, the two become one S. A counter end that is itself a curve (the band's end) is one curve, so it follows the wall.

**Per icon:** the coupe, the tree's shape and the museum keep their drawings and sizes (box heights identical); only joins, the band's ends and placement changed. Both rows ("plate rule on the cup's saucer" and "on the coupe's foot too") take the rules; older rows are untouched (history).

**Checks:** new `src/join-check.js` (curvature walk: JUMP = curvature break between curves, HUMP = a bend with a near-flat valley beside another peak, WOBBLE = a short gentle lobe between two opposite ones). This row: 37 faults before, 0 after; the other row 0. check-svg: 0 slivers, symmetric (café by design). between-checks: coupe band walls 1.38-2.25 (before 1.35-2.24, the known rim artefact), museum openings 1.25-1.50, curve jump 0.00. blur-between re-run (museum 31.7/63.6, unchanged). By eye at true size, 72px and 20-68x on every marked join plus two the check found (the museum's capital knuckle, the cup's handle foot). Only the Between board changed (every other board byte-identical).

**Canvas v223:** section "0 · The joins and the centring, as rules" at the top of the Between board (his two quotes, the three rules, before/after rows, before/after crops beside his marks). The v222 board is on Archive as `Archive-G7aq-Between` (x 5040, y 156200). Between 4376 → 7392 px; the master grid moved down to y 7992 so they don't overlap.

**Unsure:** whether the eased tips on the tree's upper tiers (now as sharp as the bottom one) read as "the same pattern" to him or as thinner; whether the band's curved ends answer "less curved than the exterior" (they now curve, which a true offset over 0.8px can't show); the star sits 0.37px lower in its slot under the same rule. His open questions (Joins, Wide → Square, which "glass … plate") are untouched.

## Turn 132: icon designer (fifth), the wobble on the line leaving a join, and the weight of the + and star (canvas v225)

Owner (v223): "can you see the wobbliness i'm talking about in these? ... on the tip of the tree join type -- further away from the point itself on both sides... and on the bowl of the cup on either side ... and on the foot of the cup... same thing not at the join itself, but on the line coming out of it. again, we need to have programmatic approaches here so that this is perfect on every icon ever created." Then: "plus *might* feel a bit light (can't exactly tell because...) the star feels heavy".

**The answer: yes.** It isn't the corner. It's how fast the bend changes as the line leaves the corner. The g7aq rounds were G2 (no step in curvature) but not G3:
- Coupe flares (bowl/stem, stem/foot): each half's curvature ramped linearly from the edge and peaked in a V at the bisector. So the rate of bend jumps where the line leaves the round (a knee) and again at the crest.
- Tree tip: one eased cubic, its handles to the corner. Its curvature stays about 0.01-0.2 for 1.7 units down each flank (2/3 of the tip), then spikes to 4.4 at the point. That gives a soft shoulder far from the tip and a hard nose. There is also a curvature step (the flank's -0.08 sweep to 0) where the ease began.
- join-check passed all of v223 (0 faults) because it reads the curvature, not its rate.

**Engine (engine.js, opts.join.fair; boards7 `JOIN8 = { ...JOIN7, fair: true, hillKeep: 'depth' }` on R2 and R2B):**
1. `hillRound`: every round, eased tips included, is a spiral designed by its curvature. Each half is a raised cosine from the edge's own curvature to a crest, with slope 0 at both ends (G3: no knee, no V). A 6x6 Newton solve finds where each half leaves its edge, each half's length, the crest and the tangent at M, so the round passes through the g7aq round's deepest point and every shape keeps its size.
   - Fallbacks, in order: 'fit' (the short side takes all its room; crest and depth move less than 0.05), then the crest at the law's radius.
   - Two passes share each edge between its two rounds, so no sliver of edge is left between them.
2. A corner running into a short bend the other way (the museum capital) becomes one S to where the bend ends, landing with the next edge's curvature. This replaces the g7aq slide pass in fair mode.
3. Each half is written as the fewest cubics that hold the hill: one; else two (Nelder-Mead on the meeting point, with a dip penalty); else 3-6 pieces on the hill. Every option is accepted against the fair check's own knee limit, and must stay one hill (monotone, read signed).
4. symD writes 5 decimals in fair mode, and rebuilds as one G2 cubic any seam crossing whose handles the booleans dropped. Output is also at 5 decimals, since 4 broke the curvature of short pieces.

Tried and dropped:
- A crest at the law's radius ('law'): the tree's tips got longer and spikier (+5.6% width). 'depth' keeps the tree's size (6.96 -> 7.03 wide, same height); its noses are a touch rounder.
- A ramp or smooth piece for drawn tangent joins (an arc running into a line): impossible without moving the arc, because a clothoid needs a shift. Not done.

**New check `src/fair-check.js`:**
- KNEE: a jump in the rate of bend over max(2, 0.25 x local crest^2).
- TAIL: a round not halfway up its bend until more than 70% of the way along.
- DIP: a round that eases off and tightens again.
- JUMP: a step in curvature, lines included.
- Steps smaller than the file's rounding are not faults.

Results:
- v223 row: 172 faults, including all three of his marks (old check: 0).
- After, the shown row: 4 faults, all at the cup's handle (drawn arcs tangent to straights; needs a drawing rule).
- After, the other row: 6 (the same 4, plus 2 tails at the ends of the domed coupe foot).
- Old join-check on the after rows: 0, except 2 HUMPs at the band end in the other row.

**Weight (`src/mark-weight.js`; numbers in svg/g7/between/mark-weight.json):**
- Blurred darkness (p80 after a 1.5px blur, at true size): star 53, + 39, a lone 1.5px line 37. Both marks use the same 1 2/3px line; the star's walls merge around its small hole.
- Rule proposed, LINE MARKS BY DARKNESS: each line mark's line is solved to one darkness T.
- Three options on the board, checked to differ at 1x and 3x:
  - A: T=39 (+ 1.67, star 1.19).
  - B: T=42.5 (+ 1.81, star 1.29).
  - C: T=46 (+ 2.06, star 1.41).
- Not applied to the row: that's his call.

**Canvas v225:**
- New section at the top of Between, "0 · The wobble on the line leaving a join". It has:
  - his quote and the answer;
  - his crops beside v223 and after, with curvature plots;
  - the plan in three rules, and the proof;
  - the before/after row, what changed, and the handle still open;
  - the weight part with options A/B/C.
- The v224 board is on Archive as `Archive-G7ar-Between` (x 5880, y 148000).
- Between is 7592 -> 8558 px tall; Master moved to y 9158.

**Checks run:**
- fair-check and join-check on every svg of both rows.
- The mark-weight solve.
- By eye at 72px, 9x and his zoom: the tree tip, both coupe flares, the band end, the museum capital.
- boards7 regenerated. Only the R2/R2B rows and the Between board change; R2's coupes and bar1 are now skipped as unused.
- Impeccable gate not run (a design board, not index.html).

**Unsure:**
- Whether the rounder noses still read as his "perfect" tree.
- Curves in the row went from 189 to 393 (his "minimal bezier curves").
- The knee threshold is calibrated on his marks (the smallest he caught: 5.7), not proven against his eye elsewhere.
- The + options are subtle at 1x.

**What's right:**

- The new check fails all three of his marks, where the old one passed them. Keep it.
- The engine rule (each round's bend rises and falls smoothly) is the right kind of answer to "programmatic ... perfect on every icon".

**What blocks a 9, by eye:**
1. **The tree's tips are now knobs.**
   - At 72px the after tree's tips read softer. At 8x and closer, the side tips end in round blobs, not points.
   - That brings back his v222 complaint ("round knobs"), on the tree he called "perfect".
   - The plot agrees: the orange hill is low and wide, so the bend starts *further* from the point than the navy did. That's the opposite of fixing "further away from the point itself".
   - The board's "a touch rounder" undersells it.
2. **The bowl-into-stem crops show the foot.** Both "v223, same place" and "after" under "the bowl into the stem" use viewBox `9.3 12.805 5.4 3`, the same as the foot section. His bowl mark has no before/after on the board.
3. **The coupe's flares look the same before and after**, both at his zoom and at 150px a unit. The fix is shown in a plot, not to his eye. The orange bowl plot also still has a step near its left knee.
4. **The handle:** "perfect on every icon ever created" can't sit next to 4 known faults in the shown row.

- "The tree tips look blobby now; I said that tree was perfect" (very likely).
- "I can't see the difference on the cup" (likely).
- "Where's the bowl?" (likely).

**Score: 7.** Fixes, in one batch:
1. **Tips:**
   - Keep v223's nose sharpness (its peak bend, the one-pattern tip).
   - Make the hill *short*, so the flank runs straight right up to it.
   - Keep the tip's length by running the flanks on, not by fattening the nose.

2. Fix the bowl crops: the right viewBox, in his framing. Check the left-knee step in the orange plot.
3. Show each join with its bend drawn on the outline itself (a comb), before beside after, so the knee shows in the shape, not only in a chart.
4. **Handle: yes, this round,** as an engine rule, not a hand redraw. A drawn bend that meets a straight starts from no bend, and the arc may shift by the tiny amount that takes. The row then has 0 faults.
5. **Curves (189 → 393): acceptable.**
   - "Minimal bezier curves" means the fewest that hold the shape, not the old count.
   - Make it a rule: one cubic per half where it holds within the knee limit, two at most.

6. **Weight:**

   - At 72px, A's star (1.19) reads spindly next to the + (1.67). Say which size the rule is solved at, and show 72px.

**Recurring blocker:** a fix is checked by its own measure (a check, a plot), and its side effect lands on a fixed point he loves (the tree, again). Engine-wide rules need a fixed-point guard. The coupe, the tree and the museum's v222 drawing must read unchanged at 72px, by eye, before anything else counts.

## Turn 134: icon designer (sixth), turn 133's fixes on v225: sharp tips that land, the handle, two curves a half (canvas v226)

I took over mid-task from the fifth designer. Every fix below is an engine rule. In boards7, `JOIN9 = { ...JOIN8, tipKeep: 'crest', tipLand: true, tipPow: 2, maxPieces: 2, arcEase: true, arcRamp: 0.3 }`, used on R2/R2B; mark-weight.js uses the same values.

**The answer (unchanged from turn 132): yes.** The wobble is a step in how fast the bend changes, just outside the corner (G2 without G3):
- At the coupe's flares it shows as knees.
- At the tree's tips it shows as a long, creeping shoulder and then a spike at the point.
- The v223 join-check read the bend itself, not its rate of change, so it passed all of these.

**The fixes, in the director's order:**

1. **The tree's tips had become knobs.**
   - Why: v223's tips peak at a bend of 19 / 11 / 7 (radius 0.05-0.15 units). The 'depth' hill kept v223's apex with a wide, low hill, so it crested at only 6.6 / 4.1 / 2.6.
   - A TIP is now any eased corner under 75°, and two rules apply to it.
   - `tipKeep 'crest'`: the tip keeps the eased tip's peak bend. On a lopsided tip that peak sits off the bisector, so the true peak is used.
   - `tipPow 2`: the hill rises as the square of the raised cosine. The bend stays near zero up the flank and gathers at the point. It is still G3, and still halfway up by 64% of the way, so there is no TAIL.
   - `tipLand`: a short hill on its own pushes every tip out toward the drawn corner. The tree grew spiky, which is why turn 132 dropped 'law'. So each tip's two edges are re-aimed (each turned and scaled about its far end) to meet just beyond v223's apex, and the tip lands where it was. This takes three passes; the landing error is at most 0.034 units (0.1px at 72).
   - Result: the crests are 18.9 / 10.7 / 6.75 (v223: 18.9 / 10.7 / 6.65), with the tips in the same places. The tree goes from 6.96 × 11.46 to 6.98 × 11.46.
   - Cost: the tips are a touch slimmer near the point, because v223's shoulders bulged out past the straight line.
   - Also fixed in hillRound: the turn is now read unwrapped (one crest solve had converged on a half that looped), and a kept crest now sets the starting length of the hill.

2. **The handle: `arcEase`.**
   - The case: a drawn arc that runs on from a line with no corner.
   - The rule: the arc's half from that node to its middle ramps up from the edge's bend over 3/10 of the way. It then holds a slightly tighter arc to the middle and lands there exactly (place, direction and bend).
   - Solved by Newton on three values (how far back along the line the ramp starts, the length, the held bend), within the edge's room.
   - Written as one or two cubics for the ramp, plus one for the arc.
   - The arc moves in by at most 0.05 units (0.15px at 72).
   - The cup: 4 JUMPs → 0.

3. **Curves: `maxPieces 2`.**
   - One cubic per half where it holds within the knee limit, else two, never more.
   - Two pieces may stray from the ideal hill by up to 0.02 units (`hillTol2`), as long as they keep the knee limit and stay one hill.
   - The two-piece fit is now also scored on how well it follows the hill's bend, so it can't stall where the pieces meet. That stall was in v225's star valleys and the coupe's band ends.
   - The row goes from 189 curves (v223) to 396. The board says so plainly and doesn't call it minimal.

4. **The board.**
   - The bowl and the foot each have their own crop, centred on the stem, with both sides shown.
   - Each join shows the bend drawn on the outline (a comb, v223 beside now) as well as the plot.
   - Each join has a "how large" figure: the most the line moves there, at 72px. The bowl moves 0.17px; the tree's tip moves 0.54px, mostly from the tip landing.
   - The board says plainly that the coupe's joins barely change shape at his zoom.

5. **Weight.**
   - A keeps the + as it is (1.67px) and only thins the star.
   - The weights are solved at true size: the + in its 26px slot, the star in its 20px slot, blurred 1.5px.
   - Each option is shown at 1x and at 72px; at 72px, A's star looks spindly.
   - Re-solved on the new star:
     - A 39: + 1.67, star 1.20.
     - B 43: + 1.82, star 1.32.
     - C 47: + 2.06, star 1.45. The + only reaches 46, and the board says so.

**New check: SHOULDER, in fair-check.**
- Walking a round's half from its foot to its crest, the rate of bend must rise once and fall once.
- It fails a stall: the rate drops under 2/5 of the lower of the peaks either side, where each of those peaks is over a quarter of the top one.
- The check walks the round on its own and passes through small stalls, so a stall can't end the round early.

**Also fixed:** boards7 `shiftD` (the optical shift for the + and the star) rounded y to 4 decimals, which put a knee in the star. It now keeps 5.

**Checks run:**
- fair-check on every row svg:
  - Shown row (R2B): **0 faults** (v223: 178; v225: 22).
  - Other row: 2 (the domed foot's TAILs, already known).
- join-check: the shown row is clean except for 2 HUMPs at the coupe's band ends. That is the counter end drawn as one curve, as in v225's other row; the fair check passes it.
- By eye at 1x, 24, 72 and 8x: the tree reads as v223's with crisper points. The coupe, cup and museum are unchanged at 72.
- boards7 regenerated.
- Impeccable not run (this is a design board, not index.html).

**Canvas v226:**
- Section 0 on the Between board is rewritten in place.
- The v225 board is archived as `Archive-G7as-Between` (x 6720, y 148000).
- The v225 svgs are frozen in `svg/g7/before-g7as/`.

**Unsure:**
- Whether the slimmer tips still read as his tree. The 72px trio is on the board.
- At 1x the A/B/C star difference is small; it shows clearly at 72px.
- The band-end HUMPs on the old check.
- tipLand re-aims edges by a hair. The shapes are kept by measure, but that is still a drawing touch made by the engine.

Merged e006dfdd and re-read the live board (v226, 9966px). Rendered it at 1x and 3x. Rendered the R2 svgs: v223 (03d218b6) beside now, at 12/24/72px, then 20x and about 135px a unit on the tree's top tip, a side tip and the star.

- **Handle:** eases in, no visible shift. Pass.
- **Tree top tip: a new wobble.**
  - The flanks run in, then bend the *other* way just under the nose, which sits on them as a small round cap: a bulb on a neck.
  - The board's own comb shows it: orange teeth (bending the other way) on both sides of the nose.
  - The plot shows the same thing: the orange rises in two steps (a shoulder on each side).
  - It reads in the board's "now" crop at about his zoom, as concave flanks pinching to a point: "further away from the point itself on both sides" again, now as an S.
  - v223 was one convex bell. Holding v223's peak sharpness *and* its depth forced the S.

   - Add INFLECT to fair-check: any sign change in the bend inside a round at a convex corner.
   - Solve the tip so its bend is never negative. It rises from the flank's own sweep, without dipping, in one hill.

   - Show the top tip and a side tip: v223, v225, now, at 72px and at his zoom, with the comb. No orange teeth near any tip.

**Recurring blocker:** each fix holds its own measure (v225: the knee; v226: v223's sharpness and depth) and pays for it somewhere the check doesn't look. The tips have now failed twice, two different ways. Guard: every round at a convex corner stays convex, enforced in the check.

**Changed since my last score?** Yes: five fixes are in, four pass by eye, and the tip changed character.

## Turn 136: icon designer (sixth), turn 135's one fix: no tip bends the other way (canvas v227)

**Why the top tip pinched.** The tree's flanks are drawn swept in, at a bend of -0.10 along their whole length; the top flanks turn about 30° that way. v223's eased round covered the top 1.2 units of each flank with a convex bend, which is the bell. v226 held v223's peak (4.4, a nose radius of 0.23) and its depth together. That left a round cap sitting on steep, swept-in sides: an S, which reads as a bulb on a neck. The side tips peak at 7-19 (radius 0.05-0.15): a point, not a cap, so they only showed a hint of it.

**Engine (boards7 `JOIN9`, mark-weight.js the same):**
- `noReverse`: a round at a convex corner never bends the other way. Where its edge sweeps the other way, the round starts from no bend and rises in one hill. The S sits at the joint, as a step of at most the edge's own 0.10 (under the 0.35 step limit), never inside the round.
- `tipSoft: 6`: a tip whose v223 peak bend is soft is not held to that peak. Its crest becomes peak² / 6, capped at the peak. A round cap on a swept-in neck is the bulb, so a soft tip is drawn as one wider hill; it ends a hair lower and wider, as turn 135 allowed. Only the top tip is soft: 4.4 → 3.2. The side tips (6.65 to 18.9) and the star's (6.8) keep their points.
  - I tried 8.8 first. The top came out at 2.2, the same dome as v225's knobs, so not that.
- `tipPow 1`: back to the plain even ramp. The square bought nothing once a tip no longer bends the other way.

**Check: INFLECT in fair-check.** Walking out from a crest that turns toward the ink, the bend may reach zero only where the round ends (at a node). A sign change inside one of the round's own curves is a fault. v226's tree: 7 INFLECT (every tip). v223: 0. Everything else in the row: 0.

**Board (section 0 only):**
- The tips figure is now rows: v223, v225, now, and "now, the bend drawn on". Each row shows the tree at 12, 24 and 72px, then the top tip, an upper side tip and a bottom tip at about his zoom.
- The rule text is "Tips: one convex hill, sharp or soft".
- The proof lists the inflect check, and says the old check's two band-end HUMPs are clean by eye at 20x, with the new check superseding the old one there.

**Checks run:** fair-check on every row svg. The shown row has 0 faults. By eye at 12/24/72 and at his zoom: the top is one dome with no neck, and the side tips are pointed. boards7 regenerated; mark-weight re-solved. The v226 svgs are frozen in `svg/g7/before-g7at/`.

**Unsure:**
- `tipSoft` 6 sits between the top tip (4.4) and the bottom tips (6.65). It is a set value for where a cap starts to read as a bulb, calibrated on this tree only.
- The top is blunter than v223's bell: v223's bell came from its convex shoulders, which were the wobble he marked. At his zoom it is a dome on tapering sides, with no neck.

## Turn 139: icon designer (seventh), the hump at the coupe's flares and the star's counter (canvas v229)

Owner (v228): "This is almost perfect. On the corner types on the coupe, there is still an awkward hump on the bowl and foot on either side as a result of the round shape. I'm not sure how to smooth it further programmatically but let's do one more try. Also think the problem with the star weight is the counter size now. I think the stroke is inner stroke so it's making the counter shape feel cramped. Let's find the right balance there for the outline icons."

**The hump, named: a shelf.** The bowl and the foot meet the stem almost level (their bend 0.08-0.16; the corner turns about 80°). The hill round was even: each half about 0.72 units, its crest on the bisector. So the bowl ran on level right up to the round, and the round sat on that short flat as a knuckle. Seen plainly at about 75-130px a unit.

**Why the check passed it:** fair-check measures how smoothly the bend changes. It did change smoothly. The check never asked where the bend sits.

**Engine rule: the leaned flare** (engine.js `opts.join.flareLean`; boards7 `JOIN10 = { ...JOIN9, flareLean: 1.75 }` on R2/R2B).
- Type: a concave round between a straight line and a gentle curve that bends the other way (bend over 0.03 and under a quarter of the round's crest).
- The round leans onto the curve: its run along the curve is 1 3/4 times its run along the line. Both runs are scaled until it crests at the corner law's own radius (coupe 2.00; the even hill crested at 2.32).
- New hillRound mode 'flare': both runs fixed, the crest point free in 2D, reached from the plain hill by continuation.
- Why 1 3/4: the even hill can lean to about 2.1:1 before its line-side half collapses. At 2 it was a 0.02-unit step on the stem. At 1 1/2 a faint shelf stayed on the foot.
- Tried and dropped:
  - longer runs with the depth free: a stout trumpet;
  - the same ink as the even round: the knuckle stays (removing the shelf needs a little ink along the curve);
  - a shorter line run with the crest moved sideways only: no solution.

**Per icon:**
- Coupe: 4 flares. They move 0.17px at most at 72px; at true size there's no visible change.
- Cup: the corner under the handle, the only other corner of this type. It moves 0.06px at 72px.
- Museum (its capitals are an S of two rounds, another type), tree, +: byte-identical.
- Star: see below.

**Check: SHELF in fair-check.** At such a round, the run onto the curve must be at least 1 1/2 times the run onto the line. v228: 5 faults (coupe 4, cup 1). Now: 0.
- INFLECT fixed: its walk now stops 4 half-widths from the crest. It had walked from the rim's round down the whole bowl into the flare. It still fails v226's tree (7).

**Outline rule.** His read was right.
- starAt fitted the star by its outside (points to the +'s arms) with the line marks' 1 2/3px. So the weight came out of the counter: 3.1px across. starRc's class rule (4px) was never used by the row.
- Rule (boards7 `starOutline` / `OUTLINE_HOLE = 2.5`; mark-weight.js `starOutlineD`): an outline mark is drawn like the solids.
  - The set's 1 1/2px line, centred on the drawn shape.
  - Sized by its counter: 2 1/2 lines across at the centre. The outside follows.
- Star: 13.2 → 14.1px across (the +'s arms are 13.2). Counter 3.1 → 4.0px across, 60% more open area. Blur darkness 53 → 47 (+ 39).
- The + is unchanged (an open mark has no counter).
- **This replaces A/B/C.**
- Seen at 1x/3x/72:
  - 2 lines: still cramped.
  - 2 2/3 lines: 14.6px, visibly big.
  - "Same size, thinner line" (1.44px): close, but it gives each outline icon its own line.

**Canvas v229:**
- Between section 0 rewritten: "The hump at the coupe's flares, and the star's counter", plus 0b, outline icons.
- The v228 board is archived as `Archive-G7au-Between` (x 8400, y 148000).
- Between is now 7423px; Master at y 8023.
- v228 svgs frozen in `svg/g7/before-g7au/`.

**Checks run:**
- fair-check on every row svg: the shown row is clean; the other row has its known 2 TAILs.
- Museum, tree and + svgs are byte-identical to v228.
- mark-weight `--outline`.
- By eye at 12/24/72 and at about his zoom.
- boards7 regenerated.
- Impeccable not run (a design board, not index.html).

**Unsure:**
- Whether the hump reads gone to him. The stem side of each flare is now short and firm.
- 1 3/4 is set by eye on the coupe and checked on the cup only.
- Whether the star is too big at 14.1px. It still measures darker than the + by blur.
